# P02 observer: local implementation and native proof

19 November 2026. **In progress; candidate disabled; no production application or schema change.**

This is the implementation continuation of the accepted observer design. The
operator authorised local implementation and focused SQL/VM proof, including
additional local database/storage if necessary. This supersedes the design
document's earlier documentation-only phase boundary. No cumulative local testing
quota applies. Existing execution receipts and the D7 reconciliation remain intact.

## Candidate changes

The SQL source adds a noncycling bigint sequence with explicit bootstrap,
transaction/session mutation-marker helpers, a read-only boundary procedure,
three lifecycle triggers and a database DDL trigger. The existing KVK publisher,
stats-report validator and maintenance root mark before their relevant work.
Existing business locks and calculations remain in place. The counter cannot be
reset through ordinary DDL while its database trigger is enabled.

`services/catalogue_observation_dal.py` validates native boundary values and exact
reviewed protocol source/options. It brackets a complete caller-owned metadata
read, supplies intermediate checkpoints, discards a changed candidate and reports
active writers separately from invalid contracts. It acquires no application
lock, starts no transaction and performs no retry. The 20 November integration
below adds explicitly selected startup and producer callers. No protected runtime
configuration selects the candidate, and native caller qualification remains open.

`services/maintenance_dal.py` includes the new session marker in entry and cleanup
checks. Its failure path releases that marker only after rollback and existing
resource release, verifies that ownership is gone, and retains custody otherwise.
The extra resource check is compatible with the predecessor SQL implementation.

The initial snapshots and lab installer are not a deployable migration. Source
closure, grants, contract projections and a source-bound migration candidate are
now prepared as described below. Committed installation issuance, the release
coordinator and deployment qualification remain incomplete.

Follow-up source tracing finds `sp_TARGETS_MASTER` calling
`sp_Prep_ExcelOutputTable`. The latter is present in the reviewed legacy source
and activation inventories and has no procedure-owned transaction around its
DROP/SELECT-INTO pair. DDL invalidation covers individual statements, but this
does not establish continuous writer presence between separately committed
statements when it is invoked directly. Resolve root/direct-call enrollment
before enabling observation; the marked transactional replacement proof above
does not close that separate route. This is source evidence, not installed-use
or native execution evidence. The source scan also confirms backfill delegates

the protected lifecycle tables; their DML therefore uses the table invalidators.

Further tracing narrows that question: `sp_TARGETS_MASTER` starts its per-KVK
publication transaction before these children, calls the enrolled
`sp_ExcelOutput_ByKVK` before `sp_Prep_ExcelOutputTable`, and commits after the
later output/view publication. Thus the normal source-defined call inherits the
transaction marker. The reviewed legacy and application grant inventories give
EXECUTE to the master, not directly to this child. This supports coverage of that
normal route; it does not establish arbitrary privileged/manual invocation or
installed-use acceptance. Manual multi-statement changes still need enrollment
or a drained migration. Do not expand the child's transaction semantics solely
because it is present in the source closure.

Before enabling the candidate, resolve and prove:

1. Direct mutation coverage, especially trigger-bypassing `TRUNCATE`/bulk routes,
   invalidator disable/re-enable, permissions and supported manual operations.
   Microsoft documents that `TRUNCATE` does not activate ordinary DML triggers;
   trigger presence alone cannot close this question.
2. Actual DDL visibility and observer engine-lock costs, with real publishers,
   report work, recovery and maintenance in both arrival orders. A logical
   interleaving or marker-only writer is not full coexistence acceptance.
3. Full comparator/lifecycle integration, same-admission coalescing/reuse,
   actual-cursor permissions, cancellation and bounded continuation.
4. Source-derived contracts, combined migration/rollback, sealed tooling and the
   exact installed predecessor rehearsal. Never hot-install additive objects
   beneath the old strict Bot contract.
5. Separate final Bot and SQL **Changes** security reviews, Deep off, against the
   completed immutable patches. Routing is review-required because this changes
   SQL, physical custody and a local execution harness; scans have not started
   while the decisive protocol implementation is still being qualified.

publisher commits and closes. The observer-first barrier is immediately after
the consumed target query and before its checkpoint. This is transaction
interleaving, not simultaneous native statement execution or whole Q03/Q04/Q07
acceptance. No existing preparation, import, archive or provider route is run.

For each case, commit evidence is written before output, state or timing
assertions. A post-commit failure stops the packet and does not replay that
publication or start the second case. The proof checks complete output evidence,
the exact new object/header/due binding, unchanged unrelated work and one fresh
250-statement observation (124 ordinary reads and 126 boundaries). The original
snapshot, all earlier failures and any newly committed fixture state are
retained. Publication uses the existing 15-second statement and 1,000 ms
lock-wait policies; observation retains its 250 ms per-query and 15-second
full-read targets. Cleanup has a three-second statement cap and unknown cleanup
retains connection custody. These are execution/cleanup safeguards and measured
performance requirements, not cumulative test allowances.

adds no transaction/session change, lock, retry or cleanup ownership. This is

remain unchanged. Native cost will include the complete combined batch, including

The revised direct rollback batch has no SELECT/result. SqlOwner.read must consume
its completion and close its original cursor, then return an empty result. Only
then does the caller execute the exact standalone SQL already used by

original-session target-lock query must return zero, and a final standalone
zero/zero read must pass. No rollback retry, relaxed active-state acceptance,
foreign connection or intervening disk write is introduced. All original
statement/request limits and post-release unblocked-overlap criteria remain.

its saved count-0/state-1 result and reproduces the refusal. The new path requires

### Deferred Optimisation
- Area: SQL dbo.sp_ExcelOutput_ByKVK KVK_RANK ordering
- Type: consistency
- Description: Equal DKP scores have no source-defined tie-breaker, so individual rank assignments can change across identical publications. Native outputs differed only within 347 zero-score ties.
- Suggested Fix: If stable tied ranks are required, have the reporting owner choose a tie policy and review downstream report expectations before changing SQL. Current P02 qualification validates the existing complete score/rank intervals and preserves all original values.
- Impact: low
- Risk: medium
- Dependencies: Reporting-domain ranking policy; separate from observer admission correction.
